## Review

**8 out of 10, Strong.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

Each slide has one assertion title, a subtitle that states the takeaway, and one clean chart with a source line (slides 5, 7, 35), so the audience can follow the argument from the data. The route is clear: an agenda on slide 2, section dividers on 3, 18 and 71, and a steady chain of unbundling examples through retail, advertising, TV and fashion. Weaknesses are the pale, small chart text and footers, many near-identical chart pages with repeated titles and similar subtitles (slides 22 to 25, 55 to 57), and the odd mix of data-scale labelling on slide 35, where the $502bn label sits on a bar capped at 100. Judgement of the full deck is partly assumed, since only 72 of 103 pages were visible.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the assertion-plus-takeaway title structure, the restrained grey, red and charcoal palette that highlights the key series, and the consistent source footers.

**Most useful repair:** Enlarge axis, legend and footer text and add direct labels or callouts on the key data point of each chart, such as the spike on slide 5 or the margin gap on slide 35.

**What would change the score:** Cut or merge repeated chart pages and fix slide 35 so the bars read in a common scale and the margin point is stated on the chart.

_Confidence medium. 72 of 103 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a title slide and an agenda of three parts: the end of free money, the new gatekeepers, and dreams for the future. The first section tests what the macro shift from near-zero rates means for tech, using charts of interest rates, ecommerce penetration, ad revenue, market capitalisation, venture investment and layoffs. The middle section argues that retail, advertising, media and fashion are being unbundled as new channels and platforms replace old gatekeepers, with Amazon, Walmart, Shein and Netflix as recurring examples. The final section looks ahead, weighing the hype cycle from the metaverse and crypto to generative AI, and ends with a conclusion listing open questions, a short About slide and a thank-you page. Most slides pair a single headline-style title with one chart or quote and a source line, so the argument is carried by data and sequence rather than dense text. Slides worth studying include the ecommerce trend-line comparison, the pair of slides on how digital pulled growth forward, and the advertising-versus-AWS margin chart.

## Questions about this deck

**How many slides does the Benedict Evans presentation deck have?**

The deck has 103 pages. Slide 2 lays out its agenda in three parts: The end of free money, The New Gatekeepers, and Dreams for the future.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about ecommerce penetration?**

Slide 5 is titled Ecommerce goes back to the trend line and says penetration grew by a third in the pandemic, and now it slows down a bit. Its chart shows ecommerce as a percentage of US retail revenue, with source US Census and seasonally adjusted data.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about advertising margins compared with AWS?**

Slide 35, titled Ads > AWS?, states that when they work, ads have much higher margins than infrastructure (let alone retail). Slide 36 quotes Walmart CEO Doug McMillon saying he cannot remember a business with the margin structure of the advertising business.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about tech layoffs?**

Slide 13, titled And all of this brings layoffs, says at least 250k tech layoffs were announced in the last 12 months, with 85k in January. Its chart shows tech layoffs announced in thousands of employees from Jan-22 to Jan-23, with source layoffs.fyi.

Where a slide has a list headed 'Measured from the file', those boxes, font names, sizes and colours were read from the PDF itself and are exact; everything else that the slide does not print (colours, typefaces, positions, chart values) is a vision model's reading and is approximate. ### Slide 5

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A clean two-line chart with a clear title, subtitle and source note makes the trend argument readable at a glance.
- Review: Each slide has one assertion title, a subtitle that states the takeaway, and one clean chart with a source line (slides 5, 7, 35), so the audience can follow the argument from the data.
- Suggested repair: Enlarge axis, legend and footer text and add direct labels or callouts on the key data point of each chart, such as the spike on slide 5 or the margin gap on slide 35.

**Text**

Ecommerce goes back to the trend line / Penetration grew by a third in the pandemic, and now it slows down - a bit / Ecommerce as % of US retail revenue / Addressable retail * / 25% / All retail & food service / 20% / 15% / 10% / 5% / 0% / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / Q4 2022 / Source: US Census. Seasonally adjusted / * Excludes cars, car parts, fuel, bars & restaurants / Benedict Evans / –– February 2023 / 5

**Layout**

Full-slide light grey (#F1F1F1) field with a white content panel spanning the middle band from about 20.5% to 86.1% of the slide height. The title is left-aligned at about x 20.5%, top band, with a serif subtitle directly beneath it; the chart is titled at x 20.6% and runs from about x 17% to x 93% with the legend in the left margin at x 4.8%. The footer sits on the grey bottom band: source lines left-aligned at x 20.5%, author, date and page number right-aligned toward x 95%.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Title: Ecommerce as % of US retail revenue. Y axis: percent of US retail revenue, 0% to 25%, gridlines every 5%, labels 0%, 5%, 10%, 15%, 20%, 25%, with light dotted horizontal gridlines. X axis: years from 2000 to Q4 2022, labelled 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020, Q4 2022; quarterly data, seasonally adjusted. Series 1, Addressable retail * (red line, about #E8453C): about 1% in 2000, about 5.5% in 2008, about 10% in 2015, about 16.5% at 2019, spikes to about 22.7% around Q2 2020, then falls to about 21.5% and stays near 21.5% to Q4 2022. Series 2, All retail & food service (dark grey line, about #4D4D4D): about 0.7% in 2000, about 3.3% in 2008, about 6% in 2015, about 10.5% at 2020 before spiking to about 15% in Q2 2020, then about 13.5%, falling to about 12.8% at Q4 2022. Both series have a thin dotted grey trend line drawn across them, rising to about 21.5% (addressable) and about 12.7% (all retail) at Q4 2022. Legend at top-left of the chart: red short line with Addressable retail *, dark grey short line with All retail & food service. No values are printed on the data points; all values above are estimates. No highlighted points beyond the peaks.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel in the middle band. Light grey background #F1F1F1 for the top and bottom bands, white #FFFFFF central panel. Title and subtitle in a serif face (MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional or Didone-like serif) in grey #545454. Chart title, axis labels and legend in MessinaSans, regular weight for labels and semibold for the chart title, grey #545454 and #535353. Footer author name in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6. Title font size about 5.56% of slide height; subtitle about 2.59%; body, labels and footer about 1.85%. Lines are thin: red #E8453C series about 2 px, dark grey #4D4D4D series about 2 px, trend lines dotted grey #6E6E6E about 1 px, gridlines dotted light grey about 0.5 px. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 6

**Text**

Ecommerce goes back to the trend line? / Which trend line? Which penetration? What about inflation? / US ecommerce revenue ($bn) / 2022 dollars / Nominal / September 2022 dollars / Nominal / 300 / 250 / 200 / 150 / 100 / 50 / 0 / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / Q4 2022 / Source: US Census, BLS. Seasonally adjusted / Benedict Evans / –– February 2023 / 6

**Layout**

Full-width header band (top about 20% of the slide) in light grey holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about x 20.5%, with the title starting at the same left edge as the chart heading. A white content band spans the middle (y 20.5% to 86%) and holds a chart with a legend at the far left (x about 4.8%) and the plot area from about x 19% to 95%. A light grey footer band (y 86% to 100%) carries the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number at right, with the page number at about x 94.9%.

**Visuals**

Line chart. Title above the plot: 'US ecommerce revenue ($bn)'. Y-axis: $bn, linear, 0 to 300, with labelled ticks at 0, 50, 100, 150, 200, 250, 300 and light dotted horizontal gridlines at each tick. X-axis: years 2000 to Q4 2022, with labelled ticks at 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020 and Q4 2022; the axis is a thin grey baseline. Series 1, '2022 dollars': dark grey (#545454) solid line; about 10 in 2000, about 50 in 2008, a dip to about 47 in 2009, about 65 in 2012, about 130 in 2017, about 165 in 2019, a steep rise to about 240 in early 2020, about 262 in 2021 to 2022 and about 262 at Q4 2022. Series 2, 'Nominal': red (#E8483F approx.) solid line; about 6 in 2000, about 37 in 2008, about 65 in 2012, about 110 in 2017, about 135 in 2019, about 160 in early 2020, a jump to about 210 mid-2020, about 240 in 2021, about 258 at Q4 2022. Two dotted dark grey trend lines (fitted curves) run from about 3 in 2000 to about 245 and about 215 at Q4 2022 respectively, with the upper one close to the 2022 dollars line. Legend at top left: dark grey dash for '2022 dollars', red dash for 'Nominal'. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands, white #FFFFFF for the content band. Title in serif (Messina Modern-style, close to a transitional serif), regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height, colour #545454. Subtitle in the same serif, about 2.6% of slide height, #545454. Chart heading and legend in a humanist sans-serif (Messina Sans style), about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height, #535353 to #545554. Source line in sans-serif regular, about 1.9%, #545454. Footer author name bold sans #A6A6A6, date in sans book #A6A6A6. Accent colours: red #E8483F for the Nominal series, dark grey #545454 for the 2022 dollars series. Lines are thin dotted grey for gridlines and trend lines; no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 7

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Grouped bars compare five markets across four dates, so the reader sees the pandemic pull-forward without any extra annotation.
- Review: Each slide has one assertion title, a subtitle that states the takeaway, and one clean chart with a source line (slides 5, 7, 35), so the audience can follow the argument from the data.

**Text**

25-50% growth pulled forward
Most major markets saw several years of future ecommerce growth compressed into the pandemic
Ecommerce sales relative to December 2018, real terms
December 2019
December 2020
December 2021
December 2022
200%
175%
150%
125%
100%
USA
UK
France
Germany
Spain
Italy
Source: US Census, ONS, Eurostat
Benedict Evans
–– February 2023
7

**Layout**

A full-width light grey header band holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about x 20.5% with a single-line subtitle beneath. A white middle band carries a chart title, a legend at the far left (x about 3%) and one vertical bar chart spanning x about 17% to 95%. A light grey footer band holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number at right, ending near x 95%.

**Visuals**

One clustered vertical bar chart. Title printed above the plot: 'Ecommerce sales relative to December 2018, real terms'. Y-axis: percentage scale from 100% to 200%, with labels 100%, 125%, 150%, 175%, 200%, and light dotted horizontal gridlines at each label. Values are index levels relative to December 2018 (100% = base). X-axis: six categories, left to right, USA, UK, France, Germany, Spain, Italy; the x-axis baseline is a thin grey line at 100%. Each category has four bars in order: December 2019 (light grey #A6A6A6), December 2020 (dark grey #535353), December 2021 (black #000000), December 2022 (red #EB483F). No values are printed on the bars, so values are estimates marked about: USA about 113% (2019), about 163% (2020), about 173% (2021), about 171% (2022). UK about 118%, about 189%, about 179%, about 147%. France about 114%, about 153%, about 131%, about 134%. Germany about 118%, about 156%, about 150%, about 138%. Spain about 138%, about 196%, about 186%, about 170%. Italy about 129%, about 177%, about 176%, about 169%. Legend at upper left, vertical list with square swatches: December 2019 light grey, December 2020 dark grey, December 2021 black, December 2022 red. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-bleed, with a white #FFFFFF band from about 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. Flat, minimal, no gradients or shadows. Header and footer are light grey #F1F1F1; middle chart area is white #FFFFFF. Title in a MessinaModern-style serif (regular, not bold), colour #545454, very large (about 5.6% of slide height). Subtitle in the same serif, about 2.6% of slide height, colour #545454. Chart title and all chart labels, legend, category labels and source in a MessinaSans-style grotesque sans-serif (regular weight), about 1.85% of slide height, colour #535353 to #545554. Footer author name in bold sans #A6A6A6, date and page number in book-weight sans #A6A6A6. Chart uses no rounded corners; bars are flat rectangles with thin white separators between adjacent bars. Gridlines are thin dotted grey lines. Baseline is a thin solid grey line. Accent colour is the red #EB483F used for December 2022 bars.

### Slide 8

**Text**

Ad revenue slows for the giants / The end of 20% annual revenue growth? / Advertising revenue ($bn, 2022 dollars) / Alphabet / Meta / 250 / 200 / 150 / 100 / 50 / 0 / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / 2022 / Source: Alphabet, Meta, BLS / Benedict Evans / –– February 2023 / 8

**Layout**

Full-bleed slide split into three horizontal bands: a light grey header band (top 0 to 20.5%) holding the title and subtitle, a white middle band (20.5% to 86.1%) holding a single line chart with a legend at the far left, and a light grey footer band (86.1% to 100%) holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned to a shared left edge at about 20.5% of slide width, which is also where the chart title and source line start; the chart plot runs from about 20.5% to 95.4% of width. The legend sits in the left margin at about 3% to 8%. The footer shows the author name, date and slide number at the bottom right, with the page number right-aligned at about 95.6%.

**Visuals**

Line chart (two smoothed curved series, no markers). Chart title: Advertising revenue ($bn, 2022 dollars). X axis: years 2000 to 2022, labelled at 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020 and 2022, no unit label. Y axis: advertising revenue in $bn (2022 dollars), range 0 to 250, labelled at 0, 50, 100, 150, 200 and 250, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled value and a solid thin grey baseline at 0. Series 1, Alphabet, red line (#E8473A), no values printed, estimates: 2000 about 0, 2005 about 9, 2010 about 38, 2015 about 83, 2020 about 165, about 228 at its 2021 peak, about 224 in 2022. Series 2, Meta, dark grey line (#4D4D4D), starts in 2010, no values printed, estimates: 2010 about 3, 2015 about 21, 2020 about 96, about 124 at its 2021 peak, about 113 in 2022. No highlighted points. Legend at upper left of the chart area: short red line segment followed by Alphabet, short dark grey line segment followed by Meta, stacked vertically with Alphabet above Meta. Line width is about 2 to 3 pixels on the 2000 pixel-wide slide. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background is a two-tone grey and white layout: header and footer bands in very light grey #F1F1F1, middle band in white #FFFFFF. Title and subtitle are dark grey #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif with high contrast and regular weight; title is large, about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%. All chart and body text is in MessinaSans, a sans-serif, in regular and semibold weights, dark grey #535353 to #545454, at about 1.9% of slide height; the chart title is MessinaSans-SemiBold at about 2.2%. Footer author name is MessinaSans-Bold in light grey #A6A6A6, date and page number in MessinaSans-Book in #A6A6A6. Accent colours are red #E8473A for Alphabet and dark grey #4D4D4D for Meta. Gridlines are light grey dotted lines, about 0.1% of slide height thick. No shadows, gradients, corner radii or rounded shapes.

9

**Layout**

A full-width title band sits at the top with the serif title and subtitle left-aligned at about 20.5% of slide width. A white panel fills the middle band from about 20.5% to 86% of slide height, holding a single line chart whose plot area starts at about 18% and runs to about 95% of width, with a legend at the far left (about 4.8%). A grey footer band carries the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right, with the page number at about 95% of width.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Market capitalisation ($bn)'. Y axis: 0 to 600 in steps of 100, unit $bn, faint dotted horizontal gridlines at each step. X axis: monthly dates from Dec-15 to Dec-22 with labels every 12 months (Dec-15, Dec-16, Dec-17, Dec-18, Dec-19, Dec-20, Dec-21, Dec-22), solid grey baseline at 0. Series 1 'Exxon' (dark grey #545454, about 3 pt smoothed line): starts about 310 in Dec-15, peaks about 410 in mid-2016, about 270 in early 2018, a trough about 110 in early 2020, about 180 at late 2020, about 280 to 300 through 2021, a peak about 555 near late 2022, ending about 495 at Dec-22. Series 2 'Zoom' (red #E8473A, about 3 pt smoothed line): starts about 25 in mid-2018, about 15 in late 2019, peaks about 145 in late 2019 and about 135 in mid-2020, falling to about 20 by late 2022. Legend at left: dark grey short line swatch with 'Exxon' and red short line swatch with 'Zoom'. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 top and bottom bands with a white #FFFFFF middle panel. Title in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif with regular weight, colour #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, about 2.6% of slide height, colour #545454. Chart title and body labels in MessinaSans-Regular, a sans-serif, about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height, colour #545454 with the chart title in SemiBold. Footer: 'Source: Bloomberg' in MessinaSans-Regular #545454; 'Benedict Evans' in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6; '–– February 2023' in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6; page number '9' in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6. Accent colour for Zoom series is red #E8473A. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

**Layout**

A grey header band fills the top fifth of the slide, with a white body panel below it and a grey footer band at the bottom. The title sits left-aligned at about x 20.5% in the header, with a serif subtitle directly beneath it. The chart title and chart start at the same left edge, a legend sits at the far left margin beside the chart, and the source line and author, date and page number sit in the footer band.

**Visuals**

Stacked column chart titled US venture investment ($bn). Y-axis runs from 0 to 100 in steps of 25, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at 25, 50, 75 and 100 and labels at the left of the plot. X-axis is labelled at every twelfth bar with Mar-10 to Mar-22; there are about 50 quarterly columns, each stacked with a red segment (Rounds for <$50m) at the bottom and a dark grey segment (Rounds for >$50m) on top. Estimated totals (about): Mar-10 about 8 (red about 6); Mar-11 about 13; Mar-13 about 12; Mar-14 about 22 (red about 12); Mar-16 about 27 (red about 12); Mar-18 about 30 (red about 15); Mar-19 about 48 (red about 16); Mar-20 about 38 (red about 17); Mar-21 about 79 (red about 23); Mar-22 about 94 (red about 28); the last columns fall to about 47 and about 36 (red about 20 and about 15). No printed values are shown on the bars. Legend at top-left of the chart: dark grey square for Rounds for >$50m and red square for Rounds for <$50m. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer, with a white #FFFFFF body panel.

### Slide 12

**Text**

The end of
free money
Source: @njashanmal
Benedict Evans
–– February 2023
12

**Layout**

Two-column split: a narrow light-grey text panel fills the left quarter (0 to 25.1% width) and a full-height photograph fills the right three-quarters (25.1 to 100% width, full-bleed top to bottom). The title sits left-aligned at the vertical middle of the left panel (starting about 25.6% from top), with the source line bottom-left at about 91% height; margins are about 4.6% from the left edge. The footer row (Benedict Evans, date, page number) runs along the bottom at about 91% height over the lower right of the photograph, with the page number at the far right.

**Visuals**

One photograph of a London bus shelter (black metal frame, glass side panels, red bench seat) on a pavement beside a road, with a tree trunk and dense green hedges behind. Two backlit advertising panels are mounted in the shelter, both branded JCDecaux. Left panel: a blue advertisement for Zapp with yellow headline 'Out of Milk?' and white 'Zapp Delivers.', small text 'Thousands of essentials to your door in minutes, 24/7.', a bunch of bananas, a milk carton, a small blue carton, a box of tea or coffee, a red apple, a box of eggs, a loaf of sliced brown bread, App Store and Google Play badges, a QR code, 'www.tryzapp.com' and the white Zapp wordmark with motion lines at bottom right. Right panel: a purple-to-yellow advertisement with the yellow 'getir' logotype at top, white 'groceries in minutes', yellow '£15 off first order', and a phone mockup showing a map, a 'getir' header, a 'Home' address bar, an '8min' delivery badge and a grid of category icons (Fruit & Veg, Dairy, Alcohol, Drinks, Baby, Pet). A London Buses roundel and the code '0120 0098' appear on the shelter roof. Lighting is soft daylight with the ad panels glowing. Dominant colours: green foliage, black shelter frame, bright blue #1E8BE0 and yellow #F5B82E on the Zapp panel, navy-purple #2E2A8C and yellow #F5B82E on the getir panel, red #D62B2B bench. Composition is a wide, eye-level shot, cropped at the left and right of the frame, full-bleed on the right three-quarters of the slide with no overlay or tint.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F2F2F2 left panel with white #FFFFFF right half, with a full-bleed photograph of a bus shelter covering the right 74.9% of the slide. Left panel background light grey #F2F2F2 covering the whole slide; right half set in white #FFFFFF beneath the photograph. Title text dark grey #545454 in a modern serif (MessinaModern-Regular, similar to a transitional serif), regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height, left aligned, set on two lines. Source line in grey #545454 in a humanist sans (MessinaSans-Regular), about 1.9% of slide height. Footer text in light grey #A6A6A6 in MessinaSans, about 1.85% of slide height: 'Benedict Evans' in Bold, date in Book, page number in Book. No accent colours, no lines, shapes, shadows or gradients; the only corner treatment is the square photograph edge.

**Layout**

Three horizontal bands: a light grey #F1F1F1 header band at the top (about 0 to 20.5% of height), a white middle band (20.5% to 86.1%) holding the chart, and a light grey #F1F1F1 footer band (86.1% to 100%). The source line sits bottom-left at x 20.5%, and the author, date and slide number sit bottom-right, ending at x about 95.5%. The slide number is 13 at the far right of the footer.

**Visuals**

Chart: vertical bar chart, single series, red bars. Chart title: 'Tech layoffs announced (000 employees)'. Y-axis: 0 to 100, labelled at 0, 25, 50, 75, 100 (unit: thousands of employees, per the chart title); dotted light grey horizontal gridlines at 25, 50, 75, 100 and a solid grey baseline at 0. X-axis: monthly categories Jan-22 through Jan-23 (13 categories), labelled in the order Jan-22, Feb-22, Mar-22, Apr-22, May-22, Jun-22, Jul-22, Aug-22, Sep-22, Oct-22, Nov-22, Dec-22, Jan-23. Values printed on the slide: none for the bars; only Nov-22 (about 52) and Jan-23 (about 85) are estimated from the axis, and the subtitle states 85k in January. Estimated values about: Jan-22 1, Feb-22 4, Mar-22 6, Apr-22 4, May-22 13, Jun-22 18, Jul-22 16, Aug-22 13, Sep-22 6, Oct-22 17, Nov-22 52, Dec-22 10, Jan-23 85. Legend: none. Highlighted points: none; all bars are the same red #EB483F. Bars are about 2.3% of slide width wide, evenly spaced. Gridline and bar positions are as given in the measured elements.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-bleed, with a white #FFFFFF band from 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. Flat, minimal style with no gradients, shadows or rounded corners. Axis and category labels are #545554 in MessinaSans-Regular, about 1.85% of slide height. Footer text is MessinaSans, about 1.85%, in light grey #A6A6A6 for the author, date and page number, and #545454 for the source line. Accent colour is red #EB483F for the bars. ### Slide 14

**Text**

Some perspective, though / Meta laid off 11,000 people in November 2022, but had hired over 15,000 since January / Employees (000) / 2010 / 250 / 2020 / 2022 / 2022/23 Layoffs / 200 / 150 / 100 / 50 / 0 / Microsoft / Alphabet / Apple / Meta / Source: Companies / Benedict Evans / –– February 2023 / 14

**Layout**

Full-width two-band slide: a grey #F1F1F1 header band at top and a grey footer band at bottom, with a white chart band between them spanning about 20.5% to 86.1% of the height. The title sits left-aligned at about x 20.5%, y 5.6%, with the subtitle directly beneath it at about y 13.3%, both sharing a left edge near x 20.5%. The chart occupies the centre-right region from about x 17% to 95%, with a legend stacked at the far left (x 4.8%). The source line sits bottom-left at x 20.5%, y 90.9%, and the author, date and slide number sit bottom-right, ending at about x 95.6%. Margins are roughly 4% on the sides.

**Visuals**

Grouped vertical bar chart titled 'Employees (000)' (title placed above the y-axis at top-left of the plot). Y axis: employees in thousands, linear scale from 0 to 250, gridlines and labels at 0, 50, 100, 150, 200, 250, drawn as dotted light grey lines. X axis: four categories, Microsoft, Alphabet, Apple, Meta, with a thin solid grey baseline at 0; the plot has room for a fifth empty category slot on the right. Series (legend order 2010, 2020, 2022, 2022/23 Layoffs): 2010 in light grey #A7A7A7, 2020 in dark grey #535353, 2022 in black #000000, 2022/23 Layoffs in red #E8473B. No values are printed on the bars, so values are estimates marked about: Microsoft 2010 about 89, 2020 about 171, 2022 about 221, layoffs about 10; Alphabet 2010 about 25, 2020 about 135, 2022 about 186, layoffs about 12; Apple 2010 about 49, 2020 about 147, 2022 about 164, no layoffs bar visible; Meta 2010 about 2, 2020 about 58, 2022 about 86, layoffs about 11. Bars are adjacent within each group with thin white gaps between them. Legend at left: four square swatches (about 2% wide) with labels stacked vertically. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from about y 20.5% to 86.1%. Clean minimal editorial style. Header and footer bands in light grey #F1F1F1, chart band in white #FFFFFF. Title in a high-contrast serif (MessinaModern-Regular, a Modern-style serif, roughly regular weight) in #545454, size about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart labels and source line in a geometric sans-serif (MessinaSans-Regular), #545454 or #535353, about 1.85% of slide height; chart heading 'Employees (000)' in MessinaSans-SemiBold #545554 about 2.2%. Footer author name in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6, date and slide number in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6, all about 1.85%. Accents: red #E8473B for layoffs series. Bars are flat with no corner radius, shadows, or gradients. Gridlines dotted, light grey.

**Layout**

Two horizontal bands: a light grey header band (top 0 to about 20.5%) holding the title and subtitle, a white content band (about 20.5% to 86%) holding the chart, and a light grey footer band (about 86% to 100%) holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned on a common left edge at about 20.5% of the width, with the chart's chart-title and axis labels sharing that edge; the legend sits in a column at about 4.8% from the left. The chart plot area runs from about 20.5% to about 95% of the width. The page number sits at the bottom right at about 94.5% of the width.

**Visuals**

Chart: clustered vertical bar chart. Chart title printed above the plot: 'Employees (000)'. Y axis: numeric labels 0, 250, 500, 750, 1,000, 1,250, 1,500, 1,750 (range 0 to 1,750, units thousands of employees, label printed as '(000)'), with faint dotted horizontal gridlines at each label. X axis: categories Microsoft, Alphabet, Apple, Meta, Amazon, baseline drawn as a thin grey line. Four series per company, in order left to right: 2010 (light grey), 2020 (dark grey), 2022 (black), 2022/23 Layoffs (red). No values are printed on the bars; estimates: Microsoft 2010 about 90, 2020 about 170, 2022 about 220, layoffs about 10; Alphabet 2010 about 20, 2020 about 135, 2022 about 190, layoffs about 10; Apple 2010 about 50, 2020 about 145, 2022 about 165, layoffs about 0 (not visible); Meta 2010 about 0 (not visible), 2020 about 55, 2022 about 85, layoffs about 10; Amazon 2010 about 30, 2020 about 1,290, 2022 about 1,540, layoffs about 15. Legend: vertical list at upper left of the chart with square swatches and labels 2010, 2020, 2022, 2022/23 Layoffs in that order. No highlighted points. Bars have thin white separators between them. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-bleed background, with a white #FFFFFF band from about 20.5% to 86% of the height. Flat, minimal, no gradients or shadows. Header and footer bands are light grey, with a white content band between them. Title in a serif face (Messina Modern Regular style, a modern serif) in dark grey #545454, large, about 5.6% of slide height, regular weight. Subtitle in the same serif, about 2.6% of height, dark grey #545454. Chart labels, source line and footer in a geometric grotesque sans (Messina Sans family) in regular weight, about 1.85% of height, grey #535353 to #545554; the 'Employees (000)' chart title is semibold, about 2.2% of height. Footer author name is bold, date and page number are book weight, all in light grey #A6A6A6. Bars: 2010 #A6A6A6, 2020 #535353, 2022 #000000, 2022/23 Layoffs red about #E8453C. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey. Baseline is a thin grey line. Sharp rectangular shapes, no corner radii.

### Slide 16

**Text**

Glass half-empty, glass half-full / How optimistic do you want to feel? / Half-empty / Over-investment and loss of discipline in the last 24 months / Uncertain macro-economic outlook / (Uncertain geo-political outlook) / Half-full / 5bn people have a smartphone now / The pandemic broke every offline habit / Every market and value chain is being remade around the internet / Benedict Evans –– February 2023 / 16

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a full-width grey header band (top about 20.5% of the height) with the title at top-left, starting at x 20.5%, and a subtitle directly beneath it. Below the header, a white body area holds two left-aligned text columns, the left starting at x 20.6% and the right at x 64.0%, separated by a dotted vertical red rule at about x 56.3%, spanning y 31.6% to 78%. A grey footer band (from y 86.1% to the bottom) carries the author name, date and page number right-aligned at the far right, with the page number at x 94.5%.

**Visuals**

One non-text element: a vertical dotted line in red (about #E84A3F) at about x 56.3% of the slide width, running from about y 31.6% to y 78% of the slide height, roughly 0.3% wide, drawn as small square dots with even spacing. It is a divider between the two columns. No photographs, charts, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background is a two-tone scheme: header and footer bands in light grey #F1F1F1 and a white central body #FFFFFF. Titles and headings are dark grey #545454 (title) and #474747 (column headings and body text). Accent is a red dotted rule (#E84A3F). Footer text is mid grey #A6A6A6. Typefaces: title, subtitle and body are MessinaModern, a serif (Book and Regular weights, column headings SemiBold); footer is MessinaSans, a sans-serif (Bold for the author name, Book for the date and number). Title is about 5.56% of slide height, subtitle about 2.59%, column headings about 3.33%, body about 3.15%, footer about 1.85%. Shapes have no corner radii, shadows or gradients.

**Layout**

Single chart slide on a three-band layout. A grey header band (top 0 to 20.5% of height) holds the title and subtitle, both left-aligned at x 20.5%. A full-width white band (20.5% to 86.1%) holds the chart, whose plot area runs from about x 19.5% to 95.3%. A grey footer band (86.1% to 100%) holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the right-hand credit line ending near x 95.6%, with the page number at x 94.6%. Left margin is 20.5% of width; right margin about 4.5%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Global computer users (bn)', placed above the plot area at the top left of the chart. Y axis: 0 to 5, unit bn (billions), labelled at 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, with dotted light-grey horizontal gridlines at each whole unit; no axis line on the left. X axis: 1980 to about 2021, labelled 1980, 1990, 2000, 2010, 2020, with a thin solid grey baseline at 0. Single series: a smooth red line starting at about 0 in 1980, about 0.03 at Apple Macintosh (about 1984), about 0.13 at Netscape (about 1994), about 0.42 at the Dotcom bubble (about 1999), about 0.9 at Facebook (about 2004), and rising to about 4.7 at the right edge (about 2021). No values are printed on the line; all values are estimates. Legend: none. Highlighted points: four solid red dots on the line at Apple Macintosh, Netscape, Dotcom bubble and Facebook, each with a dark grey text label above it (labels: Apple Macintosh, Netscape, Dotcom bubble, Facebook). No other images, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands, with a white band #FFFFFF behind the chart. Titles in dark grey #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional-style serif, regular weight. Body and chart text in dark grey #545454 to #535353 in MessinaSans-Regular, a sans-serif. Chart subtitle in MessinaSans-SemiBold #545554. Credit line 'Benedict Evans' in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6. Accent colour is red #E84335 for the data line and markers. Title text is about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle about 2.6%; chart title about 2.2%; axis, annotation and footer text about 1.85%. Line is about 3 px thick in red; gridlines are dotted 1 px light grey; baseline is a solid 1 px grey. Markers are solid red circles. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

none

### Slide 19

**Text**

The decline of old gatekeepers
Department stores dominated retail for a century. Now they’ve almost disappeared
Department store share of US addressable retail revenue*
35%
30%
25%
20%
15%
10%
5%
0%
1930 1940 1950 1960 1970 1980 1990 2000 2010 2020
Source: US Census, ARTS. Definition change in 1964
*Excluding automotive, building materials, gasoline, grocery, food service
Benedict Evans
–– February 2023
19

**Layout**

Full-width two-band layout: a light grey header band across the top (about 0 to 20.5% of slide height) holds the title and subtitle, flush left at about x 20.5%, with a white content panel between about y 20.5% and 86.1% holding the chart, and a light grey footer band at the bottom (about 86.1% to 100%) holding the source and footnote at left and the author, date and page number at right. Left margin of the text is about 20.5%; the chart's y-axis labels sit at about x 17.6% and the plot runs to about x 95.4%. Page number 19 sits at the bottom right at about x 94.5%.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Title printed above the plot: 'Department store share of US addressable retail revenue*'. Y axis: percentage share of US addressable retail revenue, labelled 0% to 35% in 5% steps, with dotted light grey horizontal gridlines at each step and a solid thin grey baseline at 0%. X axis: years labelled 1930, 1940, 1950, 1960, 1970, 1980, 1990, 2000, 2010, 2020 (annual data, range 1930 to 2020). Single series drawn as a smoothed red line about 3 px wide, colour about #E8473B. The line has a visible break between about 1964 and 1965 where the definition changed. Estimated values, none printed on the slide: 1930 about 20%; 1936 about 24.5% (local peak); 1939 about 23.8%; 1943 about 21.7%; 1946 about 17.7% (low); 1949 about 23% (local peak); 1951 about 20.4%; 1955 about 19.5%; 1958 about 18.7%; 1961 about 20%; 1964 about 21%; 1965 about 23.3%; 1967 about 25.7%; 1970 about 28%; 1972 about 28.8%; 1974 about 28%; 1977 about 29.7% (peak); 1980 about 28.7%; 1983 about 29.7%; 1986 about 29.2%; 1990 about 27.7%; 1992 about 26%; 1993 about 23.7%; 1997 about 21.8%; 2000 about 18.5%; 2003 about 15%; 2007 about 12.5%; 2010 about 10.2%; 2015 about 7.8%; 2020 about 4.2% (end point, lowest). No legend, no highlighted points, no data labels, no annotations.

**Design**

Background: flat light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands, with a pure white #FFFFFF content panel. Title in a serif typeface resembling MessinaModern Regular (a modern-style serif, regular weight), colour #545454, size about 5.6% of slide height (cap height about 4% of slide height). Subtitle in the same serif, regular, colour #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and axis labels in a geometric grotesque sans-serif resembling MessinaSans (Semibold for chart title, Regular for labels), colour #545454, about 1.9% of slide height for labels and 2.2% for chart title. Footer text in MessinaSans Regular #545454 for source lines; author name MessinaSans Bold #A6A6A6; date MessinaSans Book #A6A6A6; page number MessinaSans Book #A6A6A6. Line and shape styles: no borders or shadows on bands; gridlines are dotted 0.5 px light grey about #A0A0A0; baseline solid 0.75 px grey about #8C8C8C; data line red #E8473B, rounded joins, no markers. No corner radii, no gradients, no shadows.

### Slide 20

**Text**

And the rise of new ones / On a GMV basis, Amazon has now overtaken Walmart’s revenue / Retail revenue ($bn, 2022 dollars) / Walmart / Amazon GMV / 800 / 700 / 600 / 500 / 400 / 300 / 200 / 100 / 0 / 1970 / 1980 / 1990 / 2000 / 2010 / 2020 / Source: Amazon, Walmart, BLS, Benedict Evans / Benedict Evans –– February 2023 / 21

**Layout**

Two-band frame: a light grey header band (top 0 to 20.5%) holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at x 20.5%, and a white content panel (y 20.5% to 86.1%) holds a full-width line chart. A light grey footer band (below 86.1%) holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number at right, ending at x 95.5%. The legend sits outside the chart at the far left (x 4.8%), above the y-axis labels, and the chart plot area spans roughly x 19.5% to 95.3%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Retail revenue ($bn, 2022 dollars)'. Y-axis: 0 to 800 in steps of 100, labelled with values only, unit $bn in 2022 dollars, dotted light grey horizontal gridlines at each 100 step. X-axis: years 1970 to about 2022, labelled every decade from 1970 to 2020, solid mid-grey baseline at 0. Series 1, Walmart: dark grey line (about #4D4D4D), about 0 in 1970, about 7 in 1980, about 75 in 1990, about 290 in 2000, about 360 in 2005 to 2010 (about 360 in 2009), about 575 in 2015, about 630 peak near 2020, about 600 at the end in 2022. Series 2, Amazon GMV: red line (about #E8463A), starts about 1995 at 0, about 5 in 2000, about 35 in 2008, about 60 in 2010, about 160 in 2014, about 340 in 2018, about 580 in 2019, about 680 peak near 2021, about 675 in 2022. The lines cross at about 2019 to 2020. No data labels or highlighted points. Legend at top left of chart: short dark grey dash labelled Walmart, short red dash labelled Amazon GMV. Lines are smooth, about 2 px stroke.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 across the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Light grey (#F1F1F1) header and footer bands framing a white (#FFFFFF) content panel. Titles and body text in dark grey #545454; footer text in light grey #A6A6A6; accent colours are the red #E8463A Amazon line and the dark grey #4D4D4D Walmart line. Title is MessinaModern Regular, a transitional serif, about 5.56% of slide height, regular weight. Subtitle is MessinaModern Regular about 2.59%. Chart title and all chart and body text are MessinaSans, a grotesque sans-serif, about 1.85% of slide height; the chart title is SemiBold about 2.22%. ### Slide 21

**Text**

Software eats advertising
Digital broke up the old markets - then, a decade later, it found new models

22

**Layout**

Full-width three-band layout: a light grey header band (top 0 to 20.5%) holding the title and subtitle, a white middle band (20.5% to 86.1%) holding the chart, and a light grey footer band (86.1% to 100%) holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.5%, which leaves a blank left column of about 20% with only the chart legend in it. The chart plot runs from x 20.6% to 95.3%. Margins are about 20% on the left and 4.7% on the right. The page number 22 sits at the bottom right, and the footer text sits on the same baseline.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked area chart titled 'Advertising as % of US GDP'. Y axis: 0.00% to 1.75%, labelled every 0.25%, with faint dotted horizontal gridlines at each label. X axis: years 1930 to about 2022, labelled every 10 years from 1930 to 2020. Series, stacked bottom to top: Radio (black), TV (dark grey), Newspapers (mid grey), Magazines (light grey), Internet (red). The slide prints no data values; estimates: Radio about 0.05% in 1930, about 0.2% around 1950, about 0.05% in 2022. Newspapers is the largest series until about 1990 (about 0.6% to 0.8% of GDP for much of the period). Total advertising peaks near 1.73% in 1932, falls to about 0.57% in 1944, and reaches about 1.47% in 2000. Internet appears from about 1997, rising to about 0.6% of GDP in 2010 and about 0.85% by 2022; the total in 2022 is about 1.23%. Legend: top to bottom Internet (red swatch), Magazines (light grey swatch), Newspapers (mid grey swatch), TV (dark grey swatch), Radio (black swatch). Highlighted: the red Internet series, the only colour on the chart; the other four series are greys and black.

**Design**

Background is flat light grey #F1F1F1 at top and bottom, with a white #FFFFFF band in the middle. Title and subtitle are dark grey #545454 in a high-contrast serif (MessinaModern Regular, similar to a transitional serif), with the title at about 5.6% of slide height and the subtitle at about 2.6%. Chart title and axis labels are dark grey #545454 in a geometric sans-serif (MessinaSans, Regular and SemiBold weights), about 1.85% to 2.2% of slide height. Footer author name is bold grey #A6A6A6; date and page number are book weight grey #A6A6A6. Accent colour is red #EB483F for the Internet series only. Series colours: #000000, #535353, #7F7F7F, #A6A6A6, #EB483F. Shapes have no outlines apart from thin white separator lines between stacked areas. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are the pale, small chart text and footers, many near-identical chart pages with repeated titles and similar subtitles (slides 22 to 25, 55 to 57), and the odd mix of data-scale labelling on slide 35, where the $502bn label sits on a bar capped at 100.

A full-width grey header band holds the left-aligned title and subtitle, starting at about 20.5% from the left edge. A white band runs from about 20.5% to 86% of the height and holds the chart, with the chart heading at the top left of the plot area and the legend in a column at the far left (x about 4.8%). A grey footer band holds the source note at bottom left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number at bottom right, with the page number at about 94% from the left.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked column chart titled '2022 ad revenue ($bn)'. Y axis runs from 0 to 350 in steps of 50 ($bn, light dotted horizontal gridlines at each step). X axis categories: USA, China, UK, RoW, with a thin solid grey baseline at 0. Series (stacked bottom to top): Digital pure-play (red #EB483F), TV (dark grey #535353), All other* (mid grey #7F7F7F). Values are not printed; estimates: USA about Digital 201, TV about 71, All other about 33, total about 305; China about Digital 122, TV about 8, All other about 8, total about 138; UK about Digital 38, TV about 5, All other about 4, total about 48; RoW about Digital 180, TV about 70, All other about 80, total about 330. Legend at left, top to bottom: All other* (grey square), TV (dark grey square), Digital pure-play (red square). No highlighted points. Bars are flat with thin white separators between segments.

24

**Layout**

A full-width grey header band (top about 20% of the slide) holds a serif title and subtitle, both left-aligned on a shared left edge at about 20.5% of slide width. A white panel fills the middle from about 20.5% to 86% of the height, holding a left-aligned chart title above a single bar chart that spans about 18% to 95% of the width. A grey footer band sits below the panel: the source line is left-aligned at the same left edge as the title, and the author, date and page number are right-aligned on the right, ending at about 95% of the width.

**Visuals**

Chart: grouped vertical bar chart titled 'Top 10 global media owners by ad revenue, 2016-2021 ($bn)'. Y axis: labelled 0, 50, 100, 150, 200, 250 with units $bn implied by the chart title, range 0 to 250, dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled value. X axis: ten company categories, left to right: Alphabet, Meta, Bytedance, Alibaba, Amazon, Comcast, Microsoft, Tencent, Baidu, Disney. Each company has a cluster of six (or five) thin bars, one per year 2016 to 2021, with no printed data labels; values are estimates marked 'about'. Alphabet: about 80, 96, 117, 135, 148, 210. Meta: about 27, 40, 55, 70, 85, 115. Bytedance: about 2, 7, 17, 30, 39 (five bars). Alibaba: about 10, 15, 18, 23, 29, 32. Amazon: about 4, 6, 9, 12, 20, 32. Comcast: about 13, 14, 16, 14, 13, 15. Microsoft: about 7, 8, 9, 11, 11, 15. Tencent: about 4, 6, 8, 10, 13, 14. Baidu: about 9, 11, 12, 11, 11, 13. Disney: about 11, 11, 12, 12, 11, 12. Bars for Alphabet, Meta, Bytedance, Alibaba, Amazon, Microsoft, Tencent and Baidu are dark grey; Comcast and Disney bars are highlighted in red-orange. Bars are separated by thin white gaps and sit on a thin grey baseline. No legend.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-bleed, with a white #FFFFFF panel from about 20.5% to 86% of the height. Two-tone flat layout: light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands framing a white #FFFFFF content panel. Title text is dark grey #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular serif, regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height (cap height about 3.9%). Subtitle is dark grey #545454 MessinaModern-Regular serif at about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and axis labels are dark grey #545554 in MessinaSans sans-serif: chart title SemiBold at about 2.2%, axis and category labels Regular at about 1.85%. Source line is #545454 MessinaSans-Regular at about 1.85%. Footer author name is bold #A6A6A6, date is book weight #A6A6A6, page number is regular #A6A6A6, all about 1.85%. Accent colour is red-orange (about #EE4B3F) for the Comcast and Disney bars. Bars are dark grey #545454 with square corners; gridlines are thin dotted grey (about #8C8C8C, 0.5 px). No shadows or gradients.

### Slide 24

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are the pale, small chart text and footers, many near-identical chart pages with repeated titles and similar subtitles (slides 22 to 25, 55 to 57), and the odd mix of data-scale labelling on slide 35, where the $502bn label sits on a bar capped at 100.

**Text**

Concentration / Moving from local to global markets means moving from local to global winners / 2021 advertising ($bn) / 800 / 600 / 400 / Amazon / Meta / 200 / Alphabet / 0 / Global advertising / Ad spend by top / 100 global advertisers / Ad revenue / Source: GroupM, AdAge, companies / Benedict Evans / –– February 2023 / 25

**Layout**

Three-part vertical structure: a light grey header band across the top (0 to 20.5% of height) holding the title and subtitle, a white chart panel in the middle (20.5 to 86.1%), and a light grey footer band at the bottom (86.1 to 100%) holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned on a common left edge at about 20.5% of slide width; the chart's y-axis labels sit at about 18% and the bars occupy about 28% to 88% of width. The source line starts at about 20.5% and the page number ends near 95.5%, leaving wide side margins. Slide number 25 is printed in the footer at right (x 94.3%).

**Visuals**

Bar chart, stacked column, with title '2021 advertising ($bn)' printed above the plot at upper left. Y-axis is linear from 0 to 800 ($bn) with labelled ticks at 0, 200, 400, 600 and 800 and light grey dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled tick. X-axis is a solid thin grey baseline at 0 with three categories: 'Global advertising', 'Ad spend by top 100 global advertisers' and 'Ad revenue'. Series 1, Global advertising: single dark grey bar (#535353), top about 760 ($bn, estimate, no value printed). Series 2, Ad spend by top 100 global advertisers: single mid grey bar (#A7A7A7), top about 340 ($bn, estimate, no value printed). Series 3, Ad revenue: one stacked red column (#EB483F) split by thin white horizontal divider lines into three labelled segments from bottom to top: Alphabet (bottom, about 0 to 200, labelled inside in white), Meta (middle, about 200 to 320, labelled inside in white) and Amazon (top sliver, about 320 to 355, labelled outside to the right in dark grey). The stack total is about 355 ($bn, estimate). No legend; the three stacked company segments are identified by in-bar labels. Highlighted points: the red Ad revenue column is the only coloured series and is the emphasis; the other two bars are grey.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF chart panel in the middle band. Flat, minimal, editorial look. Header and footer bands are very light grey #F1F1F1, with the chart panel white #FFFFFF. Title 'Concentration' is in a high-contrast serif (Messina Modern Regular style, close to a modern Scotch/transitional serif), regular weight, dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height (cap height about 3.9%). Subtitle is the same serif, regular, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and all axis, category and label text are a geometric grotesque sans (Messina Sans family, close to Helvetica-like sans-serif); chart title is SemiBold #545554 at about 2.2%; tick and category labels are Regular #545554 at about 1.85%. Footer text is grey #A6A6A6 in sans: author name Bold, date Book, page number Book, all about 1.85%. Source line is Regular #545454 at 1.85%. Accent colour is red #EB483F; neutrals are #535353 and #A7A7A7. Bars have square corners and no outlines or shadows; gridlines are thin dotted light grey; baseline is a thin solid grey line. No gradients or shadows.

### Slide 25

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are the pale, small chart text and footers, many near-identical chart pages with repeated titles and similar subtitles (slides 22 to 25, 55 to 57), and the odd mix of data-scale labelling on slide 35, where the $502bn label sits on a bar capped at 100.

**Text**

Concentration / Booking and Expedia paid $13bn for reach in the last 12 months / Sales & Marketing as % revenue (TTM) / Booking / Expedia / 60% / 50% / 40% / 30% / 20% / 10% / 0% / 2010 / 2011 / 2012 / 2013 / 2014 / 2015 / 2016 / 2017 / 2018 / 2019 / 2020 / 2021 / 2022 / Source: Companies / Benedict Evans / –– February 2023 / 26

**Layout**

Two horizontal bands: a full-width grey title band across the top (about 0 to 20.5% of height) and a white content area below it (about 20.5 to 86% of height), with the grey footer strip beneath. Title and subtitle are left-aligned at x about 20.5%, sharing a left edge with the chart title, source line and footer text; the legend sits at the far left (x about 4.8%) outside the main column. The footer carries the source at left and the author, date and page number right-aligned to about 94.5%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Sales & Marketing as % revenue (TTM)'. Y axis: percent of revenue, 0% to 60% in 10% steps, dotted light-grey horizontal gridlines at each step. X axis: years 2010 to 2022 labelled at each year, baseline solid grey at 0%. Two series drawn as smoothed lines: Booking (dark grey, about #545454) starts about late 2011 at about 26% and rises to about 27% in 2012, about 29% in 2013, about 33% in 2014, about 35% in 2015, about 37% in 2016, about 40% in 2017, about 40% in 2018, about 39% in 2019, about 39% in 2020, about 43% in 2021 with a dip to about 40% at early 2021, about 43% in 2022, ending about 45%. Expedia (red, about #E84B3C) starts about mid-2016 at about 49%, peaks about 52.8% in 2018, falls to about 50% in 2019, about 48% in late 2020, dips to about 46% in 2021, rises to about 50% in 2021 to 2022 and ends about 50.5%. Legend at left: Booking with dark grey dash, Expedia with red dash. No highlighted points. Values other than the peaks are estimates.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 for the top and bottom bands, with a white #FFFFFF panel in the middle (full slide, no image or gradient). Light grey #F1F1F1 background for the title and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF content panel between them. Title and subtitle are in a serif modern face (Messina Modern style, close to a Clarendon-like transitional serif) regular weight, dark grey #545454. Chart and footer text are in a geometric sans-serif (Messina Sans style, similar to Neue Haas or Helvetica-like) regular weight, dark grey #545454; chart title semibold. Footer author name bold and light grey #A6A6A6. Title text is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, body and axis labels about 1.9%. Lines are thin: gridlines hairline dotted light grey, series lines about 0.3% of height in weight, no shadows, no gradients, no rounded corners.

**Text**

How do you reach your customers?

Logistics
How do you
reach people?
Discovery
Benedict Evans
–– February 2023

**Layout**

Single-column composition with the title and subtitle left-aligned at about x 20.5% from a common left edge, sitting in the top band. Three filled circles in a horizontal row, vertically centred at about y 51% of the slide. Left circle: dark grey #535353 with the label 'Logistics'. Between the centre and right circles is a left-pointing block arrow in light grey #A7A7A7 (triangular head pointing left, at about x 68.9%). No photographs, logos or charts. Flat fills, no shadows, no gradients.

**Design**

Background flat light grey #F1F1F1 with no pattern. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, colour #545454, about 5.6% of slide height, regular weight. Footer in MessinaSans sans-serif, #A6A6A6, about 1.9% of slide height, author name bold and the date and slide number in book weight. Gridlines light grey dotted. No shadows, gradients, rounded corners or decorative shapes.

### Slide 28

**Text**

What’s the right ad budget? / Where else could this budget be going? / 2021 ad spend as % of GDP / 1.5% / 1.0% / 0.5% / 0.0% / USA / UK / Japan / Germany / France / Spain / Italy / Source: Zenith, World Bank / Benedict Evans –– February 2023 / 29

**Layout**

Two-band layout on a full-bleed light grey field: a grey header band (top 0 to 20.5%) holds the left-aligned serif title and subtitle, a white panel (20.5 to 86.1%) holds a single vertical bar chart with its chart title at the top left, and a grey footer band (86.1 to 100%) holds the source line at left and the author, date and slide number at right. Title and subtitle share a left edge at about 20.5% of slide width, the chart's y-axis labels sit at about 17.3%, and the footer text is right-aligned to about 95.3%. Slide number 29 is printed in the footer at right.

**Visuals**

Vertical bar chart, titled '2021 ad spend as % of GDP'. Y-axis: 0.0% to 1.5%, labelled at 0.0%, 0.5%, 1.0% and 1.5%, no unit beyond the percent sign; dotted light grey gridlines at 0.5, 1.0 and 1.5; solid grey baseline at 0.0%. X-axis: seven categories, labelled USA, UK, Japan, Germany, France, Spain, Italy, left to right. Single series, values not printed on the slide; estimated from bar heights: USA about 1.33%, UK about 1.26%, Japan about 1.02%, Germany about 0.67%, France about 0.56%, Spain about 0.44%, Italy about 0.42%. The USA bar is highlighted in red #EB483F; all other bars are dark grey #535353. Bars are about 4.3% of slide width each, evenly spaced, with flat tops and no labels or legend. The chart plot area spans about x 20.6% to 95.3%, y 36% to 86%.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the full slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Clean corporate minimal style. ### Slide 29

**Text**

How much retail space do you need? / Where else could this budget be going? / Retail square feet per capita, 2021 / Shopping centres & malls / Stand-alone retail / 60 / 50 / 40 / 30 / 20 / 10 / 0 / USA / UK / Germany / Spain / France / Italy / Japan / Source: Cushman & Wakefield, Costar, ICSC, Savills, GfK, MIAC. GLA basis. / Benedict Evans / –– February 2023 / 30

**Layout**

Full-width layout with a light grey (#F1F1F1) header band across the top about 20% of the height, holding a left-aligned title and subtitle starting at x 20.5%, and a white middle band (y 20.5% to 86.1%) holding a single bar chart whose plot area runs from about x 18.5% to 95%. A light grey footer band sits below y 86.1%, with the source line left-aligned at x 20.5% and the author, date and page number right-aligned to about x 95.5%. The title and body share a left edge at about x 20.5%; the legend sits at the far left at x 2.3% to 14% in the white band, outside the plot column, and the page number reads 30 at the bottom right.

**Visuals**

Chart: vertical stacked bar chart, title 'Retail square feet per capita, 2021' (text, left-aligned above plot). Y-axis: numeric 0 to 60 in steps of 10, no unit label printed, faint dotted horizontal gridlines at each step. X-axis: seven categories, USA, UK, Germany, Spain, France, Italy, Japan, with a thin grey baseline at 0. Series: two stacked segments per bar. Top segment is 'Shopping centres & malls' (pale pink #F8DBD9 for USA; light grey about #A6A6A6 for the other countries, not in legend). Bottom segment is 'Stand-alone retail' (red #EB483F for USA; dark grey #535353 for the other countries). No data labels are printed, so values are estimates: USA stand-alone about 30.5 and shopping centres & malls about 23 (top about 53.5); UK stand-alone about 14 and top about 16.5; Germany stand-alone about 13.7 and top about 15.5; Spain stand-alone about 8.3 and top about 12; France stand-alone about 8.7 and top about 12; Italy stand-alone about 8.2 and top about 11.2; Japan stand-alone about 7.2 and top about 11.6. Legend: two entries at upper left of the plot, a pale pink square with 'Shopping centres & malls' above a red square with 'Stand-alone retail'. Highlighted point: the USA bar is the only bar drawn in the colours of the legend; the other six bars use grey tones with no legend entry.

**Design**

Background is two-tone: #F1F1F1 header and footer bands with a pure white #FFFFFF central band. Title in MessinaModern-Regular (a transitional serif, regular weight) in dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, regular, #545454, about 2.6%. Chart title and all axis, legend and footer text in MessinaSans (a geometric grotesque sans), Regular in #535353 or #545454, about 1.85% of slide height; the chart title is SemiBold at about 2.2%. Footer author name in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6, about 1.85%. Accents: red #EB483F, pale pink #F8DBD9, dark grey #535353, grey about #A6A6A6. Bars are flat rectangles with square corners and no shadow or outline; gridlines are thin dotted grey lines; baseline is a thin solid grey line.

### Slide 30

**Text**

Retail versus logistics / The US had over-invested in retail space - now the trend reverses / Annual US construction spending ($bn, 2022 dollars) / Retail & food service / Warehouses / 100 / 75 / 50 / 25 / 0 / 1970 / 1980 / 1990 / 2000 / 2010 / 2020 / Source: US Census, BLS / Benedict Evans / –– February 2023 / 31

**Layout**

Two horizontal bands frame a white chart panel: a light grey header band (top 0 to about 20.5% of height) holding the title and subtitle, and a light grey footer band (about 86% to 100%) holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned on a common left edge at about 20.5% of slide width, leaving a wide left margin; the legend sits at the far left (about 4.8%) outside the chart column. The chart plot runs from about 20.6% to 95.2% of width, and the page number 31 sits at about 94.5% to 95.5% at the bottom right, with a right margin of about 4.5%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Annual US construction spending ($bn, 2022 dollars)'. Y axis: US$ billions in 2022 dollars, range 0 to 100, labelled ticks at 0, 25, 50, 75, 100, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled tick. X axis: years 1970 to 2022 (ticks labelled 1970, 1980, 1990, 2000, 2010, 2020), with a thin solid grey baseline at 0. Series 1, 'Retail & food service', dark grey (#545454) smoothed line about 2.5pt wide: about 53 in 1973, about 35 in 1975, about 55 in 1979, about 42 in 1983, about 76 in 1985, about 62 in 1990, about 47 in 1991, about 59 in 1995, about 71 in 2000, about 62 in 2003, about 86 in 2007 (peak), about 33 in 2010, about 53 in 2016, about 41 in 2019, about 33 in 2021, about 37 in 2022. No data labels printed; values above are estimates. Legend at upper left of chart: short dark grey dash then 'Retail & food service', short red dash then 'Warehouses'. No highlighted points. Source line at bottom left; no logos, photographs or icons.

### Slide 32

**Text**

Retail as media / ‘Merchant media’ - Amazon had $38bn of ad revenue in 2022 / Amazon advertising, TTM ($bn) / 'Other, predominantly ads' / Ads / 40 / 30 / 20 / 10 / 0 / Mar-17 / Sep-17 / Mar-18 / Sep-18 / Mar-19 / Sep-19 / Mar-20 / Sep-20 / Mar-21 / Sep-21 / Mar-22 / Sept-22 / Source: Amazon / Benedict Evans –– February 2023 / 33

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) slide split into three horizontal bands: a grey header band holding the left-aligned title and subtitle starting at about 20.5% from the left, a white central band (about 20.5% to 86% of the height) holding the full-width line chart, and a grey footer band with the source line at left and the author, date and slide number at right. The title sits top-left of the header; the chart's left edge aligns with the title at about 20.5% of the width; the right margin of the chart and footer is about 5% of the width; the slide number is at the far right of the footer.

**Visuals**

One line chart titled 'Amazon advertising, TTM ($bn)'. Y-axis: $bn, range 0 to 40, labelled ticks at 0, 10, 20, 30, 40, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each tick. X-axis: monthly time from Mar-17 to Sept-22, labelled every six months (Mar-17, Sep-17, Mar-18, Sep-18, Mar-19, Sep-19, Mar-20, Sep-20, Mar-21, Sep-21, Mar-22, Sept-22), with a thin grey baseline. Series 1, 'Other, predominantly ads', is a red dotted line from about Mar-17 at about 3.5 rising to about 10 at Sep-18, about 15 at Mar-20, about 20 at Sep-20, ending near Aug-21 at about 30.5. Series 2, 'Ads', is a solid red line starting about Nov-20 at about 26, about 29 at Sep-21, about 32 at Mar-22, about 34 near Jun-22 and about 38 at Sept-22 (the subtitle states $38bn for 2022). Legend at top left: a red dot marker labelled 'Other, predominantly ads' above a short red dash labelled 'Ads'. No data labels and no highlighted points. Source line reads 'Source: Amazon'. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands, pure white #FFFFFF for the chart band. Title and subtitle in grey #545454, a serif typeface similar to Messina Modern Regular (a transitional serif, regular weight). Chart title in a semibold sans-serif similar to Messina Sans SemiBold, colour #545454. Axis labels and legend in a regular sans-serif similar to Messina Sans Regular, colour #535353 to #545554. Title text is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle and chart title about 2.2% to 2.6%, axis and body text about 1.9%. Accent colour red about #E8473F for both chart series. Gridlines thin light grey dotted; baseline thin mid-grey solid. Footer author name in bold #A6A6A6, date and slide number in book weight #A6A6A6. No shadows, gradients or corner radii.

**Layout**

Full-width grey (#F1F1F1) slide with a white content band across the middle; title and subtitle sit top-left in one left-aligned column starting at about x 20.5%, with the chart indented to the same left edge. The source line sits at the left of the grey footer band at the same x, and the author, date and page number are right-aligned in the footer, ending at about x 95.6%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Amazon revenue, TTM ($bn)'. Y axis: 0 to 40 in steps of 10, unit $bn, dotted light-grey gridlines at 10, 20, 30 and 40, solid grey baseline at 0. X axis: quarterly-style labels from Mar-17 to Sept-22 at six-month intervals (Mar-17, Sep-17, Mar-18, Sep-18, Mar-19, Sep-19, Mar-20, Sep-20, Mar-21, Sep-21, Mar-22, Sept-22). Series 1 'Prime' (dark grey #545454 solid line, about 2 px): about 7 at Mar-17, about 13.5 at Sep-18, about 20 at Mar-20, about 24 at Sep-20, about 27.5 at Mar-21, about 31 at Sep-21, about 32.5 at Mar-22, about 35.3 at Sept-22; no printed values. Series 2 'Ads' (red #E8473F solid line): starts about 26 around mid-2021, about 31 at Mar-22 and about 38 at Sept-22; no printed values. Series 3 'Other, predominantly ads' (red dotted line): about 3.5 at Mar-17, about 10 at Sep-18, about 15 at Mar-20, about 20 at Sep-20, about 28 at mid-2021, ending about 30.5 near Sep-21; no printed values. The Ads and Other lines both lead into the Prime line around Sep-21 to Mar-22 where they converge; the Ads line finishes above Prime. Legend at upper left of the chart with a small red dot, a red line and a dark grey line, labels as in text. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for the top and bottom bands with a white #FFFFFF content band between y 20.5% and 86.1%. Titles and subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, colour #545454 (dark grey). Body and chart labels in MessinaSans-Regular sans-serif in #545454 or #535353. Chart title in MessinaSans-SemiBold #545554. Accent colour red #E8473F for Ads and Other series, dark grey #545454 for Prime. Footer author name in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6, date in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle and chart title about 2.2 to 2.6%, body and axis text about 1.85%. Lines are thin with no shadows, corner radii or gradients.

35

**Layout**

Full-width light grey (#F1F1F1) header band across the top with the title and subtitle left-aligned at about 20.5% from the left edge, sharing a common left edge. A white content panel spans the full width between about 20.5% and 86.1% of the slide height. The chart fills the panel, starting at about 20.6% from the left, with the legend in the left margin at about 4.8%. A light grey footer band sits below the panel, with the source line at the left content edge and the author, date and page number right-aligned at about 94.3% to 95.5%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Global advertising revenue ($bn, 2022 dollars)'. Y-axis: 0 to 200, labelled 0, 50, 100, 150, 200 (no unit label on axis; unit given in chart title as $bn, 2022 dollars). X-axis: 1980 to about 2022, labelled 1980, 1990, 2000, 2010, 2020. Dotted light grey horizontal gridlines at 50, 100, 150 and 200; solid grey baseline at 0. Series 1, Newspapers, dark grey (#545454) line: about 103 in 1980, about 102 in 1982, about 123 in 1985, about 152 in 1989, about 137 in 1993, about 152 in 1997, peak about 170 in 2000, about 150 in 2003, about 148 in 2007, about 110 in 2009, about 89 in 2012, about 63 in 2016, about 45 in 2019, about 28 in 2022. Series 2, Amazon, red (#E8443A) line: starts about 5 in 2017, about 10 in 2018, about 15 in 2019, about 30 in 2021, about 37 in 2022. The two lines cross at about 2021. Values are estimates marked 'about' because the slide prints no data labels. Legend at upper left of chart: dark grey short line with 'Newspapers', red short line with 'Amazon'. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white (#FFFFFF) panel from 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. Light grey (#F1F1F1) background with a white content panel. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, dark grey #545454, approx 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, #545454, approx 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and axis labels in MessinaSans sans-serif, semibold for chart title and regular for labels, #545454, approx 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height. Footer text in MessinaSans, #A6A6A6 for author, date and page number, with author in Bold and date in Book weight; source line in #545454 Regular. Lines are thin: the series strokes are about 0.3% of slide height wide; gridlines are dotted 0.1% grey; baseline is solid thin grey. No shadows, gradients or corner radii.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

- shape at x 0, y 0, w 100, h 100 [#F1F1F1]: Full-slide background fill (Covers the slide, no border.)
- shape at x 0, y 20.5, w 100, h 65.6 [#FFFFFF]: White content panel (Full-width band behind the chart, no border.)
- title at x 20.5, y 5.6, w 36.1, h 5.6 [MessinaModern-Regular, serif, regular weight; font size 5.56% of slide height; #545454; left aligned]: Old and new gatekeepers
- subtitle at x 20.6, y 13.3, w 22.1, h 2.6 [MessinaModern-Regular, serif, regular weight; font size 2.59% of slide height; #545454; left aligned]: New ad models overtake old ones
- chart at x 20.5, y 35, w 75, h 46 [#545454]: Line chart: Global advertising revenue ($bn, 2022 dollars), Newspapers and Amazon series, y-axis 0 to 200, x-axis 1980 to about 2022 (Dotted gridlines at 50, 100, 150, 200 in light grey; solid grey baseline at 0. ### Slide 35

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A simple bar comparison with a short footnote lets an analytical claim about margins stand on its own visually.
- Review: Each slide has one assertion title, a subtitle that states the takeaway, and one clean chart with a source line (slides 5, 7, 35), so the audience can follow the argument from the data.
- Review: Weaknesses are the pale, small chart text and footers, many near-identical chart pages with repeated titles and similar subtitles (slides 22 to 25, 55 to 57), and the odd mix of data-scale labelling on slide 35, where the $502bn label sits on a bar capped at 100.
- Suggested repair: Enlarge axis, legend and footer text and add direct labels or callouts on the key data point of each chart, such as the spike on slide 5 or the margin gap on slide 35.
- What would change the score: Cut or merge repeated chart pages and fix slide 35 so the bars read in a common scale and the margin point is stated on the chart.

**Text**

When they work, ads have much higher margins than infrastructure (let alone retail)

**Layout**

Full-width light grey header band (top about 20% of the slide) holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at x about 20.5%. A white chart panel fills the middle band from y 20.5% to 86.1%, with the chart title and a legend in the left column (x about 4.8%) and the plot area from x about 18% to 95%. A light grey footer band below y 86.1% holds the source and footnotes at x 20.5% (left-aligned) and the author, date and page number at the right, ending at x about 95.5%. The slide number 36 sits at far right of the footer, on the same baseline as the author credit.

**Visuals**

Clustered vertical bar chart titled 'Amazon segments, 2022 ($bn)'. Y-axis: $bn, linear, range 0 to 100, labelled ticks at 0, 25, 50, 75, 100, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each tick. X-axis: three categories, Group, AWS, Ads, with a thin baseline at 0. Three series per category, in this order: Net sales (dark grey #535353), Operating income * (pale pink #F8DBD8), Capex ** (red #EB483F). Labelled value: Group Net sales $502bn (printed inside the bar top in white). Other values are not printed; estimates: Group Operating income about 12, Group Capex about 64; AWS Net sales about 80, AWS Operating income about 23, AWS Capex about 32; Ads Net sales about 37, Ads Operating income about 22, Ads Capex about 2. Legend at left of the chart, stacked vertically: dark grey square with 'Net sales', pale pink square with 'Operating income *', red square with 'Capex **'. No series is highlighted beyond the red capex colour.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands, white #FFFFFF for the chart panel. Titles dark grey #545454 in a serif typeface (MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional serif, regular weight), title size about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Body and chart labels in a grotesque sans (MessinaSans-Regular, regular weight), #535353 to #545454, about 1.85% of slide height. Chart title in MessinaSans-SemiBold, #545554, about 2.2%. Footer author name in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6. Accent red #EB483F for the capex series. Bars are flat with no outlines or shadows; gridlines are thin dotted grey.

The footer runs along the bottom right, outside the panel: author name, dash, date, then slide number at the far right margin.

none

The footer is set in a small grey sans-serif (MessinaSans) at about 1.9 percent of slide height: the name in bold #A6A6A6, the date and number in book weight #A6A6A6. ### Slide 37

**Text**

New channel, new gold rush / First party data, inventory, and very attractive margins / Walmart / $2.7bn / in 2022 / Uber / $500m / run rate / Amazon / $38bn / in 2022 / Inventory / 1P Targeting / Margins! / Source: Companies / Benedict Evans –– February 2023 38

**Layout**

Single slide with a left-aligned title block at top and a horizontal row of four equal circles across the middle, all on one shared baseline. The title and subtitle start at about 20.5% of slide width, which is the same left edge as the source line and the four circles' first column. The footer sits near the bottom: the source line at bottom left, and the author, date and page number right-aligned to about 95% of width. The left margin is about 20% and the right margin about 5%, so the layout is off-centre to the right, with the left 20% empty.

**Visuals**

Four circles in a single row, with equal diameters and even spacing. Circle 1 is dark grey #535353 and reads 'Walmart / $2.7bn / in 2022'. Circle 2 is dark grey #535353 and reads 'Uber / $500m / run rate'. Circle 3 is dark grey #535353 and reads 'Amazon / $38bn / in 2022'. Circle 4 is red #EB483F and reads 'Inventory / 1P Targeting / Margins!'. Each circle is a flat filled disc with no outline, no shadow and no gradient, and holds white centred text. The circles are not charts, photographs or logos. No axes, legend or data points are present.

**Design**

Background: Flat solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat, minimal, editorial look on a light grey ground. Titles and subtitle are set in a serif face, close to Messina Modern Regular, in dark grey #545454. The circle names are in Messina Modern SemiBold and the circle values in Messina Modern Book, both in white #FFFFFF. The source line and footer are a sans-serif face, close to Messina Sans, in dark grey #545454 for the source and light grey #A6A6A6 for the footer, with the author name bold. Title text is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle and circle text about 2.6% to 3.0%, and footer text about 1.9%. Shapes are plain circles with no corner radius, no shadows, no gradients and no strokes. The single red circle is the only accent colour on the slide.
